---

> Monetization method where ad networks bid in real-time for ad placements within an app.

- Published: 2024-01-11
- Modified: 2025-12-08
- URL: https://www.mobileaction.co/glossary/what-is-in-app-bidding/

Monetization method where ad networks bid in real-time for ad placements within an app.

In-app bidding, also known as header bidding for apps, is a programmatic advertising technique that enables multiple ad demand sources to compete simultaneously for ad inventory within a mobile app. It brings the concept of header bidding, which originated in web advertising, to the in-app advertising ecosystem. How does it work? In-app bidding involves the following key steps: Integration: The app developer integrates an in-app bidding SDK (Software Development Kit) provided by an in-app bidding platform or technology provider into the mobile app. This SDK facilitates communication between the app and the various demand sources participating in the bidding process. Ad Request: When an ad placement becomes available within the app, instead of sending the ad request to a single ad network or exchange, the app sends the request to the in-app bidding SDK. Auction: The in-app bidding SDK conducts an auction among multiple demand sources, such as ad networks, exchanges, or demand-side platforms (DSPs). Each demand source submits their bid for the ad impression in real-time. Bid Evaluation: The in-app bidding SDK evaluates the bids received from the demand sources based on predefined rules and criteria. This evaluation may consider factors such as bid price, targeting parameters, ad quality, and historical performance. Winner Determination: The highest bid wins the auction, and the winning demand source is notified through the SDK. The winning demand source's ad creative is then delivered to the app for display. Ad Display: The app displays the winning ad creative to the user, ensuring a seamless...

> Fraudulent activities manipulating install metrics. Protect your campaigns from Install Fraud.

- Published: 2024-01-11
- Modified: 2025-12-08
- URL: https://www.mobileaction.co/glossary/what-is-install-fraud/

Fraudulent activities manipulating install metrics. Protect your campaigns from Install Fraud.

Install fraud, also known as app install fraud, refers to deceptive practices aimed at artificially inflating the number of app installations or downloads. It involves fraudulent techniques employed by individuals or automated bots to generate fake installs, leading to inaccurate metrics and misleading data for app developers, advertisers, and app stores. Types of install fraud There are several common types of install fraud that app developers and advertisers should be aware of: Click Injection: In click injection fraud, malicious apps or malware installed on a user's device detect when a legitimate app is being installed. They then generate fake ad clicks just before the installation completes, taking credit for the install and falsely attributing it to the fraudulent source. Click Spamming: Click spamming involves flooding ad networks or attribution platforms with an excessive number of clicks in a short period. This overwhelms the tracking systems and makes it difficult to accurately attribute the installs to the correct sources. Device Farms: Fraudsters set up device farms, which are large groups of devices or emulators, to simulate real users and generate fake installs. They may use automated scripts or bots to download and install apps on these devices, giving the appearance of genuine user activity. Install Hijacking: In install hijacking, fraudsters intercept the app installation process and redirect it to their own fraudulent app. This way, they can claim credit for the install and deceive advertisers or app developers. SDK Spoofing: SDK spoofing involves manipulating the software development kit (SDK) integration within...

> A type of fraud where malware intercepts legitimate app installs to steal attribution, impacting marketing analytics and budgets.

- Published: 2024-01-11
- Modified: 2025-12-08
- URL: https://www.mobileaction.co/glossary/what-is-install-hijacking/

A type of fraud where malware intercepts legitimate app installs to steal attribution, impacting marketing analytics and budgets.

Install hijacking is a type of fraudulent activity where malicious actors intercept the app installation process and redirect it to their own malicious app. Instead of the user downloading the intended legitimate app, they unknowingly end up downloading a different app, typically one that is malicious or unwanted. Install hijacking can occur through various channels, such as app stores, third-party app stores, or even websites. How it works? Interception: Malicious actors inject code or manipulate the installation process to intercept the user's attempt to download a legitimate app. This can happen when a user clicks on a download link or initiates an app installation. Redirection: The malicious actors redirect the user to a different app, often one that looks similar to the intended legitimate app. They may create a fake app storefront or use deceptive techniques to make the user believe they are downloading the right app. Installation of Malicious App: The user proceeds with the installation, believing they are getting the legitimate app. However, they end up installing the malicious app instead. This app may contain malware, adware, spyware, or other unwanted features that can harm the user's device or compromise their privacy. Negative consequences Malware Infections: The malicious app that users unknowingly install can contain harmful code, such as malware, that can compromise the user's device, steal personal information, or perform unauthorized activities. Localization: Internationalization lays the groundwork for localization, which is the process of adapting a software application or product to a specific language, region, or locale. > Prevent keyword cannibalization from hurting your app store rankings. Learn how to identify conflicting keywords across your app listings, understand the impact on visibility, and implement effective resolution tactics.

- Published: 2024-01-11
- Modified: 2025-12-08
- URL: https://www.mobileaction.co/glossary/what-is-keyword-cannibalization/

Prevent keyword cannibalization from hurting your app store rankings. Learn how to identify conflicting keywords across your app listings, understand the impact on visibility, and implement effective resolution tactics.

Keyword cannibalization refers to a situation where multiple pages or pieces of content on a website are competing against each other for the same target keyword or set of keywords in search engine rankings. When multiple pages are optimized for the same keyword, it can lead to confusion for search engines and hinder the overall visibility and performance of the website in search results. Some key points Duplicate Content: Keyword cannibalization often occurs when multiple pages on a website have similar or identical content targeting the same keyword. This can confuse search engines, as they may struggle to determine which page should be ranked higher for the given keyword. Ranking Dilution: When different pages on a website compete for the same keyword, it can dilute the overall ranking potential of the website. Instead of consolidating the relevance and authority of the website around a specific keyword, the competing pages may divide and weaken the overall ranking power. Confused User Experience: Keyword cannibalization can also lead to a confusing user experience. If multiple pages on a website appear in search results for the same keyword, users may find it difficult to identify the most relevant page or determine which page to visit. This can result in a poor user experience and reduced engagement. Content Optimization: To address keyword cannibalization, it is important to review and optimize the content across the website. This may involve consolidating similar pages, merging content, or reorganizing the website's structure to ensure that each page targets a...

> Learn the optimal keyword density for app store listings in 2026. Discover how to balance keyword usage, avoid over-optimization penalties, and create metadata that ranks well while remaining user-friendly.

- Published: 2024-01-11
- Modified: 2025-12-16
- URL: https://www.mobileaction.co/glossary/what-is-keyword-density/

Learn the optimal keyword density for app store listings in 2025. Discover how to balance keyword usage, avoid over-optimization penalties, and create metadata that ranks well while remaining user-friendly.

Keyword density refers to the percentage or ratio of how frequently a target keyword or phrase appears in a piece of content compared to the total number of words in that content. It is often used as a metric to assess the relevance and optimization of a webpage for a specific keyword. Some key points Calculation: To calculate keyword density, you divide the number of times a keyword appears in a piece of content by the total number of words in that content and then multiply the result by 100 to get the percentage. For example, if a keyword appears 10 times in a 500-word article, the keyword density would be (10/500) x 100 = 2%. Importance: In the past, keyword density was considered an important factor in search engine optimization (SEO) as it was believed that higher keyword density would improve a page's ranking for that keyword. However, search engines have evolved to focus more on the overall quality, relevance, and user experience of content rather than just keyword density. Keyword Stuffing: Excessive and unnatural repetition of keywords solely for the purpose of manipulating search engine rankings is known as keyword stuffing. Keyword stuffing is considered a black hat SEO technique and can lead to penalties from search engines. It is important to avoid keyword stuffing and maintain a natural and readable flow of content. Context and Relevance: Search engines now place more emphasis on the context and relevance of keywords within content. Rather than focusing solely on keyword...

Use keywords in a way that maintains the flow and readability of the content. Aim for a natural and conversational tone. Meta Description: While not a direct ranking factor, including the target keyword in the meta description can encourage click-through rates from search engine results pages. This can provide additional relevancy signals to search engines and make the URL more user-friendly and memorable.
